For example, George Washington served two consecutive terms and is counted as the first president, instead of the first and second, but Grover Cleveland and Donald Trump are counted twice because their two terms were not consecutive. A vice president who temporarily becomes [[Acting President of the United States|acting president]] under the [[Twenty-fifth Amendment to the United States Constitution|Twenty-fifth Amendment]] to the Constitution is not counted, because the president remains in office during such a period.&lt;!-- Citation not required; general note about the table. --&gt;&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-16" data-mw-group="lower-alpha" id="mwig"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwiw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwjA">[</span>a<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwjQ">]</span></span></a></sup></th>

When they did develop, during Washington's first term, Adams joined the faction that became the [[Federalist Party]]. The elections of 1792 were the first ones in the United States that were contested on anything resembling a partisan basis.{{Sfnmp |''Guide to U.S. Elections''|2010|1pp=197, 272|Nardulli|1992|2p=179}}&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-23" data-mw-group="lower-alpha" id="mwxQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwxg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwxw">[</span>c<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwyA">]</span></span></a></sup></td></tr>

Federalist John Adams was elected president, and Jefferson of the Democratic-Republicans was elected vice president.{{Sfnp |''Guide to U.S. Elections''|2010|p=274}}&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-26" data-mw-group="lower-alpha" id="mw6w"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw7A"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw7Q">[</span>d<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw7g">]</span></span></a></sup></td></tr>

When [[Andrew Jackson]] became president in 1829, this group became the \&quot;Anti-Jackson\&quot; [[Opposition (politics)|opposition]], and organized themselves as the [[National Republican Party]].{{Sfnmp|1a1=''Guide to U.S. Elections''|1y=2010|1p=228|2a1=Goldman|2y=1951|2p=159}}&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><a href="#cite_note-JQAdams-34" data-mw-group="lower-alpha" id="mwAYo"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAYs"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAYw">[</span>f<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAY0">]</span></span></a></sup></td>
